font-family:sans-serif;'><br /><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>f.<\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Quorum and Necessary Vote<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>1.<\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Quorum<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Four members  of  the  Historic  Resources  Commission  shall  constitute  a  quorum.  No  official <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>business of the commission shall be conducted without a quorum present.<br /><\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>2. Voting<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>An  affirmative  vote  of  the  majority  of  commission  members  present  and  constituting  a  quorum <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>is required for all decisions of the Historic Resources Commission.<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><br /><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>g.<\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Attendance<\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'> <\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>In accordance with Section 2-35 of the City of Fayetteville Code of Ordinances, a Historic <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Resources  Commission  member  shall  attend  at  least  75  percent  of  regularly  scheduled  commission <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>meetings  held  annually  from  the  date  of  the  member's  appointment.  Any  member  who  fails  to <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>comply   with   this   requirement   shall   be   automatically   removed   from   the   Historic   Resources <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Commission.  The  Chair  of  the  Historic  Resources  Commission  shall  notify  the  City  Council  if  a <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>member fails to comply with this attendance requirement.<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><br /><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>h.<\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Rules of Procedure<\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'> <\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>The Historic Resources Commission shall adopt rules of procedure governing its <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>procedures and operations. Copies shall be made available for public inspection in the Planning and <\/span><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'>Zoning Division.<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><br /><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><br /><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span style='font-size:20px;'><b><u>30-2.C. Standards and Requirements for Development Applications<\/u><\/b><\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>This  section  includes  the  individual  review  procedures,  standards,  and  related  information  for  each  of  the <\/span><span>applications for development permits and approvals, as summarized in Table 30-2.A.2, Development Review <\/span><span>Structure.<\/span><span style='font-size:20px;'>30-2.C.8. <\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span style='font-size:20px;'>Certificate of Appropriateness<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>a. <\/span><\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Purpose<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>The  purpose  of  this  section  is  to  provide  for  the  review  of  development,  construction,  alteration,  or <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>demolition     of     landmark     historic     structures     and     other     historic     structures     within     the <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Historic/Landmark  Overlay  District  by  the  Historic  Resources  Commission  in  accordance  with  the <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>procedures  and  standards  of  this  Ordinance  and  the  Design  Guidelines  for  Fayetteville's  Historic <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Districts and Local Landmarks.<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>b. <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Applicability<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'><br /><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>1. <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>General<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>a. <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Unless  otherwise  exempted  by  Section  30-2.C.8.b.2,  Exemptions,  no  exterior  portion  of  any <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>building   or   other   structure,   including   masonry   walls,   fences,   light   fixtures,   steps   and <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>pavement,  other  appurtenant  features,  any  aboveground  utility  structure,  or  any  type  of <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>outdoor  advertising  sign  shall  be  erected,  altered,  restored,  moved  or  demolished  on <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>designated    landmark    historic    structures    or    other    historic    structures    within    the <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Historic/Landmark  Overlay  District,  and  no  Building  Permit  for  such  development  shall  be <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>issued,  until  an  application  for  a  Certificate  of  Appropriateness  as  to  exterior  features  is <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>submitted  to  and  approved  by  the  Historic  Resources  Commission  in  accordance  with  this <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>section.<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>b. <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Where a Certificate of Appropriateness is required for exterior work that does not require a <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Building Permit, no work shall occur until the project is submitted to, and receives a written <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Certificate of Appropriateness from, the Historic Resources Commission in accordance with <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>this section.<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'><br /><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>2. <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>Exemptions<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>The following is exempt from the requirements of this section:<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>a.<\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>The  ordinary  maintenance  or  repair  of  any  exterior  architectural  feature  that  does  not <\/span><span style='font-family:sans-serif; font-size:18.3333px;'>involve a change in design, materials, or outer appearance;<\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>b. <\/span><span>The  ordinary  maintenance  or repair  of streets, sidewalks,  pavement markings, street signs, <\/span><span>or traffic signs;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>c. <\/span><span>The  construction,  reconstruction,  alteration,  restoration,  or  demolition  of  any  of  the  above <\/span><span>features  where  the  City  Manager  certifies  the  activity  is  required  for  the  public's  safety <\/span><span>because of an unsafe or dangerous condition; and<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>d. <\/span><span>The maintenance of any aboveground utility structure or the immediate restoration of such <\/span><span>a structure in the event of an emergency.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>c. <\/span><span>Initiation <\/span><span>An  application  for  a  Certificate  of  Appropriateness  shall  be  initiated  by  any  person  having  authority <\/span><span>to   file   an   application   in   accordance   with   Subsection   30-2.B.1,   Authority   to   File   Applications.<br /><\/span><span>d.<\/span><span>Procedure<\/span><span>1.<\/span><span>Basic Procedures<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>Except  as  modified  by  Sections  30-2.C.8.d.2\u20147  below, <\/span><span>procedures   and   requirements   for   the   submission, <\/span><span>completeness <\/span><span>determination, <\/span><span>review, <\/span><span>recommendation, <\/span><span>hearing, <\/span><span>and <\/span><span>decision <\/span><span>on <\/span><span>applications   are   as   established   in   Section   30-2.B, <\/span><span>Common Review Procedures.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>2.<\/span><span>Review and Action on Minor Works by City Manager<\/span><span>a.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>Where the development proposed by a Certificate <\/span><span>of   Appropriateness   is   identified   by   the   Design <\/span><span>Guidelines  for  Fayetteville's  Historic  Districts  and <\/span><span>Local  Landmarks,  which  is  hereby  incorporated <\/span><span>by  reference,  as  one  that  would  not  impair  the <\/span><span>integrity   of   the   property   and/or   district   as   a <\/span><span>whole,  the  City  Manager  may  act  to  approve  the <\/span><span>application  or  approve  it  subject  to  conditions, <\/span><span>based   on   the   standards   in   Section   30-2.C.8. <\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>e, <\/span><span>Certificate of Appropriateness Standards. The City <\/span><span>Manager   shall   forward   to   the   commission   any <\/span><span>staff-approved    Certificate    of    Appropriateness <\/span><span>application involving minor works.<\/span><span>b.<\/span><span>In  all  other  instances,  following  staff  review,  the <\/span><span>City    Manager    shall    submit    all    Certificate    of <\/span><span>Appropriateness    applications    to    the    Historic <\/span><span>Resources Commission for review and action.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>3.<\/span><span>Review  and  Action  by  Historic  Resources  Commission<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>For <\/span><span>all <\/span><span>other <\/span><span>Certificate <\/span><span>of <\/span><span>Appropriateness <\/span><span>applications,    following    staff    review,    the    Historic <\/span><span>Resources  Commission  shall  conduct  an  evidentiary <\/span><span>hearing on  the  application  in  accordance  with  Section <\/span><span>30-2.B.12,  Public  Notification,  and  Section  30-2.B.14, <\/span><span>Evidentiary <\/span><span>Hearing <\/span><span>Procedures <\/span><span>(Quasi-Judicial <\/span><span>Decisions).   After   close   of   the   hearing,   the   Historic <\/span><span>Resources  Commission  shall  consider  the  application, <\/span><span>relevant  support  materials,  the  staff  report,  the  City <\/span><span>Manager's  comments,  and  any  testimony  or  evidence <\/span><span>given  at  the  hearing  and  included  in  the  record.  The <\/span><span>Historic Resources Commission, by a majority vote of a <\/span><span>quorum present, shall take one of the following actions <\/span><span>based    on    the    standards    in    Section    30-2.C.8.e, <\/span><span>Certificate of Appropriateness Standards:<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>a. <\/span><span>Approval of the application as submitted;<br /><\/span><span>b. <\/span><span>Approval of the application subject to conditions; or<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>c. <\/span><span>Denial of the application<\/span><span>4.<\/span><span>Demolition<\/span><span>a.<\/span><span>The   Historic   Resources   Commission   may   not   deny   a   Certificate   of   Appropriateness <\/span><span>application  proposing  the  demolition,  removal,  or  destruction  of  a  designated  landmark  or <\/span><span>building,  structure,  or  site  within  the  Historic/Landmark  Overlay  District  unless  the  State <\/span><span>Historic  Preservation  Officer  determines  that  the  building,  structure,  or  site  is  of  statewide <\/span><span>significance  and  its  owner  would  not  suffer  extreme  hardship  or  be  permanently  deprived <\/span><span>of all beneficial use or return if demolition were denied.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>b. <\/span><span>The Historic Resources Commission, however, may delay the effective date of a Certificate of <\/span><span>Appropriateness  for  such  demolition,  removal,  or  destruction  for  up  to  365  days  from  the <\/span><span>date of approval. The Commission shall use such time to negotiate with the owner to find a <\/span><span>means of preserving the building, structure, or site.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>5.<\/span><span>Conditions of Approval <\/span><span>In  approving  a  Certificate  of  Appropriateness  application,  the  Historic  Resources  Commission <\/span><span>may  impose  appropriate  conditions  on  the  approval  in  accordance  with  Section  30-2.B.16, <\/span><span>Conditions of Approval.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>6.<\/span><span>Appeal<\/span><span>a.<\/span><span>If the decision involves property owned by the State, the State shall have the right to appeal <\/span><span>the  decision  to  the  State  Historical  Commission  upon  filing  such  an  appeal  within  30  days <\/span><span>after the date of the decision. The decision of the State Historical Commission shall be final <\/span><span>and binding upon both the State and the Historic Resources Commission.<\/span><span>b.<\/span><span>An  appeal  from  the  City  Manager's  decision  on  a  Certificate  of  Appropriateness  application <\/span><span>for minor works or from the Historic Resources Commission's decision on other Certificate <\/span><span>of  Appropriateness  applications  shall  be  filed  within  ten  business  days  of  the  date  of  the <\/span><span>decision,  and  shall  be  reviewed  and  decided  by  the Zoning  Commission  in  accordance  with <\/span><span>Section 30-2.C.18, Appeal, except that the appeal shall be in the nature of certiorari.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>7.<\/span><span>Expiration<\/span><span>a.<\/span><span>The  Historic  Resources  Commission  may  prescribe  a  time  limit  within  which  either  the <\/span><span>activity   or   the   building   permit   for   the   development   authorized   by   the   Certificate   of <\/span><span>Appropriateness  shall  be  secured.   Failure  to  establish  the  activity  or  obtain  the  building <\/span><span>permit shall void the Certificate of Appropriateness. <\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>b. <\/span><span>Unless   otherwise   specified   in   the   Certificate   of   Appropriateness,   the   Certificate   of <\/span><span>Appropriateness shall automatically expire within  one  year  after  the date  of issuance if the <\/span><span>activity or building permit authorized by the Certificate is not secured.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>c. <\/span><span>In  cases  where  a  maximum  time  frame  for  development  is  established  as  a  condition  of <\/span><span>approval,  the  Certificate  of  Appropriateness  shall  expire  upon  the  lapse  of  the  allowable <\/span><span>time frame.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>8.<\/span><span>Extension<\/span><span>Upon  written  request  submitted  at  least  30  days  before  expiration  of  the  time  period  provided  in <\/span><span>accordance with Section 30-2.C.8.d.7 above, and upon a showing of good cause, the city manager may <\/span><span>grant  one  extension  not  to  exceed  six  months.  Failure  to  submit  a  written  request  for  an  extension <\/span><span>within  the  time  limits  established  by  this  section  shall  result  in  the  expiration  of  the  Certificate  of <\/span><span>Appropriateness<\/span><span>.<br /><\/span><span>a.<\/span><span>Certificate of Appropriateness Standards<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>1. <\/span><span>A  Certificate  of  Appropriateness  application  shall  be  approved  upon  a  finding  the  application <\/span><span>complies  with  the  Design  Guidelines  for  Fayetteville's  Historic  Districts  and  Local  Landmarks, <\/span><span>and is otherwise congruous with the special character of the Historic/Landmark Overlay District.<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>2.<\/span><span>The  following  design  features  and  elements  shall  also  be  considered  in  reviewing  Certificate  of <\/span><span>Appropriateness applications:<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>a.<\/span><span>Lot coverage - the percentage of the lot area covered by primary structures;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>b.<\/span><span>Setback - the distance from the lot lines to the building (setback);<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>c.<\/span><span>Building height;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>d.<\/span><span>Spacing of buildings - the distance between adjacent buildings;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>e.<\/span><span>The  proportion,  shape,  positioning,  location,  pattern,  sizes,  and  style  of  all  elements  of <\/span><span>fenestration and entry doors;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>f.<\/span><span>Surface materials and textures;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>g.<\/span><span>Roof shapes, forms, and materials;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>h.<\/span><span>Use of regional or local architectural traditions;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>i.<\/span><span>General  form  and  proportion  of  buildings  and  structures,  and  the  relationship  of  additions <\/span><span>to the main structure;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>j.<\/span><span>Expression of architectural detailing;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>k.<\/span><span>Orientation of the building to the street;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>l.<\/span><span>Scale  -  the  size  of  units  of  construction  and  architectural  details  in  relation  to  the  human <\/span><span>scale,  and  the  relationship  of  the  building  mass  to  adjoining  open  space  and  nearby <\/span><span>buildings and structures;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>m.    Proportion of width to height of the total building façade<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>n.<\/span><span>Archaeological sites and resources associated with standing structures;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>o.<\/span><span>Effect of trees and other landscape elements;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>p.<\/span><span>Major landscaping that would impact known archaeological sites;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>q.<\/span><span>Style, material, size, and location of all outdoor advertising signs;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>r.<\/span><span>Appurtenant features and fixtures, such as lighting;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>s.<\/span><span>Structural condition and soundness;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; font-family:sans-serif;'><span>t.<\/span><span>Walls  and  their  physical  ingredients,  such  as  brick,  stone,  or  wood  walls,  wrought  iron <\/span><span>fences, evergreen landscape masses, or combinations of these;<\/span><\/span><\/div><div><span style='font-size:18.3333px; Note that some of the names listed are generic, or they may not have a name at all.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Description <\/span><span>- A description of the site or district.  Note that not all sites will have a detailed description.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>APN(s) <\/span><span>- Parcel number of the site, or multiple parcel numbers if the site contains multiple parcels.  Some sites are within right-of-ways and may not have a parcel number.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Address or Location <\/span><span>- an address or a description of the location.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Year(s) Built <\/span><span>- The year a structure was built.  Not all built data is available, or may not be applicable.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Status Code / Description <\/span><span>- The status code and description for a site as established from the Office of Historic Preservation (OHP).  For list, click <\/span><a href='https://ohp.parks.ca.gov:443/pages/1068/files/Resource-Status-Codes.pdf' style='text-decoration:underline;' rel='nofollow ugc'><span>here<\/span><\/a><span>.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Potential Themes <\/span><span>- For a Historic Context Statement and Survey, themes are a way to categorize patterns of historical development.  More information about this and of Historic Contexts and Surveys in general is <\/span><a href='https://ohp.parks.ca.gov:443/?page_id=23317' style='text-decoration:underline;' rel='nofollow ugc'><span>here<\/span><\/a><span>.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Architectural Style <\/span><span>- Describes the architectural style of the buildings, where applicable.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Source <\/span><span>- Source agency or database of the site or district.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Source Date <\/span><span>- The date the source material was extracted.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Year Designated <\/span><span>- The year a site or district was officially added to a County, State, or National Registry.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Mills Act Contract <\/span><span>- Whether a site is under a Mills Act contract which is an agreement between a property owner and a local government to preserve the historic resources on their property and receive property tax relief.  More information from OHP is <\/span><a href='https://ohp.parks.ca.gov:443/?page_id=21412' style='text-decoration:underline;' rel='nofollow ugc'><span>here<\/span><\/a><span>.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Jurisdiction <\/span><span>- Identifies whether the site or district is in a city or unincorporated area.  Most of the sites in this layer will be in unincorporated areas, but, there are sites within cities that are official County Landmarks (like the Hollywood Bowl).<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>City / Unincorporated Community <\/span><span>- Identifies the city or community name of the site or district.  See Jurisdiction note above for cities.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Notes <\/span><span>- Explanatory notes about a site - mostly references to the source materials.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>File Location (Hyperlink) <\/span><span>- Hyperlinks to source materials.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>County Landmark / District Number <\/span><span>- Identification number for the official LA County.  More information is <\/span><a href='https://planning.lacounty.gov:443/long-range-planning/historic-preservation-program/landmarks-and-historic-districts/' style='text-decoration:underline;' rel='nofollow ugc'><span>here<\/span><\/a><span>.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>County Designation Date<\/span><span> - Date that a landmark or district was adopted.  More information is <\/span><a href='https://planning.lacounty.gov:443/long-range-planning/historic-preservation-program/landmarks-and-historic-districts/' style='text-decoration:underline;' rel='nofollow ugc'><span>here<\/span><\/a><span>.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>CA_TYPE / CA_NUM / CA_DATE <\/span><span>- These are the official types, ID numbers, and dates for those sites in the California Registry.  Visit the California Historical Resources page <\/span><a href='https://ohp.parks.ca.gov:443/ListedResources/?view=county&amp;criteria=19' style='text-decoration:underline;' rel='nofollow ugc'><span>here<\/span><\/a><span>.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>NALANDMARK / NA_TYPE / NA_NUM / NA_DATE <\/span><span>- These are the official types, ID numbers, and dates for those sites in the National Registry.  A link to the National Register of Historic Places is <\/span><a href='https://npgallery.nps.gov:443/nrhp' style='text-decoration:underline;' rel='nofollow ugc'><span>here<\/span><\/a><span>.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Legend <\/span><span>- These are the general categories of sites and districts that are used in the symbology of GIS-NET.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Fire Damage <\/span><span>- Flags sites that have structures that were damaged or destroyed from major fires with the following categories per Cal Fire:<\/span><\/p><ul><li><p><span>No Damage: sites within or near a fire perimeter that were not damaged by the fire listed<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span>Affected: 1 - 9% damage<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span>Minor: 10 - 25 % damage<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span>Major: 26 - 50% damage<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span>Destroyed: over 50% damage<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span>Inaccessible: site unable to be assessed<\/span><\/p><\/li><\/ul><\/li><li><p><span style='font-weight:bold;'>Fire Damage Notes <\/span><span>- Lists the name of the fire, and flags those sites that have multiple structures with multiple damage categories.<\/span><\/p><\/li><\/ul><p style='margin:0 0 0 0;'><span /><\/p><p style='margin:0 0 0 0;'><span style='font-weight:bold;font-size:14pt'>Update History:<\/span><\/p><p style='margin:0 0 0 0;'><span /><\/p><ul><li><p><span>1/13/22 - added 'Holmes House' in Altadena that was adopted by the BOS on 2/9/21.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span>6/23/22 - added 'Unique Theatre' (County landmark), and 'Chicano Moratorium March August 29, 1970' (State and Fed district) - both in East Los Angeles.<\/span><\/p><\/li><li><p><span>7/26/22 - added 'Jackie Robinson Park' (County landmark) in Antelope Valley. Whatcom Million Trees Project is a 501c3 non-profit in Bellingham, Washington with the mission to plant and protect more than one-million trees in Whatcom County -- to enhance the health and resilience of local communities.<\/p><p>This map is based on the latest lidar aerial mapping data (early 2023) from the WA Department of Natural Resources. Most trees on this map are mature native conifers, some of the oldest in Bellingham. Each neighborhood also contains smaller extraordinary trees, many of which are identified in an excellent species-based <a href='https://www.google.com/maps/d/u/0/viewer?mid=1qD5BT2hxynqIePn-9twqKJYpvZJhUAA&amp;ll=48.74665080407924%2C-122.48071982626574&amp;z=13' rel='nofollow ugc'>Bellingham Trees map<\/a>. <\/p><p>The City's new <a href='https://cob.org/wp-content/uploads/Landmark-Tree-Emergency-Ordinance.pdf' rel='nofollow ugc'>Landmark Tree Ordinance<\/a> automatically protects most trees 36&quot; dbh (diameter at breast height) or larger. Our map is a tool to locate and identify such trees -- on public and private lands. Enjoy!<\/p><p> <\/p><p><i>Tip: Expand the legend and layers icons [shown above] that are in the map. By integrating various datasets, including those from Open Data DC and the U.S. Census Bureau, the project provides detailed visualizations that illustrate the intricate relationships between these elements and their impact on the community's well-being.<\/p><p>Key components of the project include:<\/p><p>Tree Canopy Analysis: Mapping the distribution and health of over 2.4 million trees across DC, highlighting their ecological benefits such as air quality improvement and urban heat island mitigation.<br />Cherry Trees and Parks: Exploring the significance of cherry trees, particularly around the Tidal Basin, and detailing the features and importance of the top 20 national parks in DC. The project showcases how these green spaces contribute to environmental health and community cohesion.<br />Historic Walking Routes: Providing guided routes from the White House to various landmarks, such as the National Mall and the Jefferson Memorial, detailing what visitors can see in 5-minute increments up to 30 minutes.<br />Public Safety Insights: Using the Crime Incidents in 2023 dataset to overlay crime statistics on the green spaces map, examining trends and identifying potential areas for safety improvements through environmental design.<br />The project also includes practical safety tips for visitors, ensuring they can enjoy their explorations of DC's parks and historic sites with confidence.
